# Cade Skywalker

- **Aliases:** Cade
- **Era:** Legacy
- **Continuity:** default Legends; entries present for: Canon, Legends
- **Research tier:** MASTERY (Legacy-era version only); DEEP
- **Entries:** 1755 (943 with quotations; 904 marked as fragments; recounted after the public-release cleanup). Rebuilt 2026-11-17 from 2309 old entries; old file: `characters/_bak/cade-skywalker.md.bak-20261117`.
- **How to read:** each entry is the quotation in full (block quote), then its source and point of view. "Note" lines are summaries from our notes, not quotations. `[fragment — full passage not on file]` means the source text is not on the box, so only the words in our notes are shown. Debate and forum material is in the last section and is unverified.

# LEGENDS (Legends / EU — main focus)

## The Complete Star Wars Encyclopedia, Vol. I (A–G)

> 260 Roan Fe/ Fel finally agreed to take on the challenge of uniting the GA military and ending the war with the Confederation. Fel, Maraslah The beautiful daughter and only child of Emperor Roan Fel, the Princess was considered to be her father's eventual successor, al­ though the Moff Council was not happy with the idea of a woman ruling the New Empire. Marasiah Fel, known to her confidantes as Sia, had beentrained asan Imperial Knight, but had to flee into hiding during an Imperial mission on So­ corro when Darth Krayt took over the galaxy by force. Eventually she was tracked down by Darth Talon, but escaped an initial assassination attempt. In fleeing, she met Cade Skywalker and his bounty hunter colleagues, who helped her escape to Vendaxa. Darth Talon attacked again, but Cade and his friends defeated the Sith. They were soon joined by two of her father's closest aides . . . and then four more Sith Lords, one of whom almost killed Soontir Fe/ the Princess with lightsaber blows. Cade Skywalker saved her life with the help of the Force. The two of them re­ alized that the Jedi and Imperial Knights needed toabandon their long-simmering antagonism and join together if there was ever to be a chance to defeat the new Sith Empire.
— Source: scan pages 241–280; Darth Talon; Cade Skywalker.

> Bantha and his clan are the closest thing to family  for Cade. He never knew his mother, though by now he has at least met her, and at some point he had to find Bantha after the fall of Ossus. John and I knew a lot about Bantha, but what kind of woman would he find compatible? Droo is a rare, one of a kind woman who can kill a man a dozen different ways before breakfast, as well as make a mean bilaberry patoga. The 'kits' were also  fun to invent and each has their own personality. I see the Rawks as another layer in Cade's life where he can go when things get too hot.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #101 (2008).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Cade Skywalker — entry/section: Duursema: the Rawks: "Says artist Jan Duursema, "Bantha and his clan are the closest thing to family  for Cade. John and I knew a lot about Bantha, but what kind of woman would he find compatible? Droo is a rare, one of a kind woman who can kill a man a dozen different ways before breakfast, as well as make a mean bilaberry patoga. The 'kits' were also  fun to invent and each has their own personality. I see the Rawks as another layer in Cade's life where he can go when things get too hot." —Jan Duursema, Star Wars Insider 101, p. 61" Source: *Star Wars Insider #101* (2008), scan p.61

> Luke could turn Vader only because of the ties between them. We've seen just how merciless and dangerous Krayt can get when mad. Cade understands that, so long as Krayt lives, the Sith Order will be intact and Cade will never know peace. Cade is not concerned about redeeming Krayt or about saving the galaxy, when you come right down to it. He wants people to leave him alone. He wants to live his life on his own terms, not anyone else's. Krayt needs to die. Now, whether or not he can do that is an entirely different question. Cade's gotten himself in over his head before. We'll see if it happens again.
— Source: Star Wars Insider #101 (2008).
Note (our notes, around the quote): Luke Skywalker; Darth Vader (Anakin Skywalker); Cade Skywalker; Darth Krayt — entry/section: Ostrander: Krayt needs to die: "Adds Ostrander, "Luke could turn Vader only because of the ties between them. We've seen just how merciless and dangerous Krayt can get when mad. Cade understands that, so long as Krayt lives, the Sith Order will be intact and Cade will never know peace. Cade is not concerned about redeeming Krayt or about saving the galaxy, when you come right down to it. He wants people to leave him alone. He wants to live his life on his own terms, not anyone else's. Krayt needs to die. Now, whether or not he can do that is an entirely different question. Cade's gotten himself in over his head before. We'll see if it happens again." —John Ostrander, Star Wars Insider 101, p. 62" Source: *Star Wars Insider #101* (2008), scan p.62
![Quote image: *Star Wars Insider #101* (2008), scan p.62 — full quote as printed](quotes/ins101-s62-adds-ostrander-luke-could-turn-vader.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/038-star-wars-insider/101-ins101.md:690`

> Descendant of Anakin and Luke Skywalker. Trained as a Jedi, he has since turned his back on his legacy.
>
> Once apprenticed to the pirate Rav, Cade found a home in the underworld of the galaxy working as a bounty hunter and smuggler.
>
> Cade has dropped the name 'Skywalker.' Not even his closest associates know Cade was a Jedi.
— Source: Star Wars: Legacy #0 — "Welcome to the Future..." (Jun 2006; 25¢ preview/primer issue); > "CADE SKYWALKER" /.

What it shows (our notes): (p22–23: Cade, already infected and turning, heals himself and Azlyn of a disease Celeste calls incurable. The art shows Force-lightning-like healing energy. Memories of their apprentice days on Ossus play across p23. p24: both walk out cured. This is a major healing feat. Speaker check p23: "THE DARK SIDE AT WORK!" is red-bordered, so Muur. The others are Celeste's.)
> Roorgli has her Comet Broom insignia torn off, clutched in her hand. It has a small compartment on its backside, the side that would have been against her suit. In it is a copy of the Cometary Water Reclamation file info, which may be found in the computer core section (DC 15) below. This is actually a plant by the assassin, who wishes the data found, but there is really nothing to indicate that it was planted. The Dark Force user took the original insignia. Mronk’s does not have such a compartment, of course, and looks to be of slightly thinner and different manufacture, if anyone checks. Any Force User who specifically tries to See Force here, or who opens up his/her Senses, definitely gets a strong impression of the Dark Side at work. That is, this was not just an evil act; there was a Dark Side Force user present. Room contents/damage: The DC number before the slash is for a Force User with Alter, or Knowledge (Forensics or some crime-scene-related skill). The higher DC, after the slash, is for Search. At Your Service Page 15 DC Information Gathered 5/10 Someone really tossed everything loose around, but they deliberately trashed the computer.
— Source: Star Wars: Legacy #28 — "Vector, Part 9 of 12" (Dark Horse, September 2008).

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Major lore: Nyna Calixte = Morrigan, Cade's mother and Kol's love; Kol knew her new identity ~7 years before the present (flashback during the Wayland failure). Cade once attacked her with Force lightning (reference to earlier arc). Calixte secretly works to warn Roan Fel. This confirms p11 hangar officer as Calixte (still flag p11 as by appearance).
  — Source: Star Wars: Legacy #44 — "Monster, Part Two" (Jan 2010).

> I’m still not accustomed to space travel.” Artwo can talk, too, and tries to calm the other, but adds, “You’re a mindless, useless philosopher.” C3PO climbs into an emergency lifepod. R2D2 is persuaded to join him by increasingly violent explosions. The lifepod is ejected.
>
> Only two starships are left in the attack. On the planet below, Skywalker is told that the King and his convoy have been destroyed and that the Senate has traitorously called an end to the war.
>
> Only two starships are left in the attack. On the planet below, Skywalker is told that the King and his convoy have been destroyed and that the Senate has traitorously called an end to the war. He orders the two remaining starships to break off the attack.
>
> They do so—but are destroyed as they retreat.

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Major lore: Maladi (birth name Malincha, Devaronian-born child of a Jedi; parents killed by Krayt) claims she — not Zenoc Quah alone — corrupted the Ossus Project on Wayland (reconcile with #44 p3; Quah likely her instrument — flag). Maladi gave Nihl his prosthetic hand. Maladi arranged Cade's presence on Wayland (so Rav's bounty was a Sith setup). Nihl = former Nagai warlord.
  — Source: Star Wars: Legacy #45 — "Monster, Part Three" (Feb 2010).

— Source: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Corrections, 2026-10-21 (from the page-by-page re-read of *Legacy Vol. 3: Claws of the Dragon*, owner material 013, where 013 pN = 009 p(N+301); each was checked on zoomed crops): (1) p436: Deliah does not fly the *Mynock* or catch Cade; Chak is flying, Deliah is on the guns, and Syn's hand catches Cade at the hatch. (2) p338: the torturer is Darth Maladi, not Talon; "NO, YOU USE THE FORCE TO PURGE YOURSELF… LIKE A SITH…" and "I SUPPLY THEM." are Maladi's, and "THEN HE REALIZES THE LIMITATIONS OF THE JEDI TEACHINGS." is the arriving Krayt; the crop was renamed `p338-maladi-cade-purges-drug-like-sith.jpg`. (3) p331: "I AM HERE ON ORDERS FROM OUR LORD KRAYT. SKYWALKER IS WANTED ALIVE." is Talon, not Nihl. (4) p341: "…HE'S GOT DELUSIONS OF ADEQUACY." is Syn, not Deliah. (5) p377: "AND IF KRAYT DOES DIE, WHO WILL LEAD THE SITH ORDER?" is Maladi and "NO HEIR HAS BEEN NAMED…" is Nihl. (6) p382: "SKYWALKER'S ARROGANCE WILL BE HIS UNDOING…" is Krayt, not Wyyrlok. (7) p413: "THEN TAKE THIS SABER AND STRIKE ME DOWN!…" names no addressee; Cade holds the offered saber, so "Krayt to Hosk" is changed to addressee UNCLEAR, most likely Cade. (8) p417 plot wording: the Sith recoil from Cade's burst; they aren't scattered or knocked down. See `013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md`, "Differences vs 009".
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:12`

> holds off Darth Krayt in a prolonged lightsaber duel, taxing Krayt into stasis
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
>
> battle with Skywalker has cost me
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: (debate #81) — Owner Material 009 — Star Wars: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): Krayt is never in danger from Cade. The exchange is one or two blocks, a taunt and a rage strike, then a blaster hit from outside and Cade jumps out of the window. Krayt is also explicitly holding back, since he needs Cade alive (p146, p346, p413: "YOU TOLD ME YOU NEED ME TO HEAL YOU"). legends-feats.md says (debate #81) Cade "holds off Darth Krayt in a prolonged lightsaber duel, taxing Krayt into stasis". Book 1 doesn't show that. In Claws the duel is brief, and the "battle with Skywalker has cost me" line is Legacy #27 (outside this book). Cade does perceive Krayt's illness ("WHAT FIGHTING ME IS COSTING YOU"), which a Krayt-side debater would call a sickness caveat, not a measure of Cade's power.
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:331`

> TK-throws a large multi-passenger shuttle into Darth Talon
> [fragment — full passage not on file]

> many hundreds of yards
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: (debate #61) — Owner Material 009 — Star Wars: Legacy Book 1 (omnibus): power-scaling notes with panels; Scaling (ANALYSIS): this is the.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Scaling (ANALYSIS): this is the "TK-throws a large multi-passenger shuttle into Darth Talon" feat in legends-feats.md (debate #61). On the page, Talon throws the wreck (part of the destroyed Jedi ship) first. Cade stops it and reverses it with a push that carries Talon away. The size is a ship-section/shuttle-scale wreck. On-panel distance is not quantified ("many hundreds of yards" is a debater's estimate). Untrained and unwilling, Cade overpowers a Hand's telekinesis by reflex.
Our notes: `notes/materials/009-legacy-book-1.md:390`

## Star Wars Saga Edition: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels

> falls while fighting off a torrent of Sith attackers, including Darth Nihl *(p196)*
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Jedi numbers: Wolf Sazen is CL15 (UtF +19), but his build is purely defensive and utility, with no battle strike and no multi-attack. Kol Skywalker is CL14 with UtF +20 and Strong in the Force, and "falls while fighting off a torrent of Sith attackers, including Darth Nihl" (p196). Early-series Cade is only CL11: UtF +12 per the Force Powers line (+11 per Skills), lightsaber +11, plus dark transfer.
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p196 — full quote as printed (panel)](011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p196-kol-skywalker-bio-nihl.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide.md:39`

> By the end of the Sith-Imperial War, Darth Krayt declares himself Emperor. However, his body suffers the effects of having lived far beyond its natural lifespan. The Yuuzhan Vong implants used to extend his life begin to consume him. Furthermore, healing trances and Force healing become less effective. Darth Krayt knows that his time is limited unless a new solution is found. At one point he captures Cade Skywalker, an heir to the Skywalker bloodline gifted with incredible healing powers, in the hopes that one day he will be able to harness those same powers to prolong his life.
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Bio, continued (p170).
![Quote image: Legacy Era Campaign Guide (WotC 2009), p170 — full quote as printed (panel)](011-legacy-era-campaign-guide-panels/p170-krayt-bio-dying-cade-healing.jpg)

> to closely monitor the training of his disciples, including the brief training received by Cade Skywalker on Coruscant
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels; Training droids: Krayt uses Roving Eye droids.

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): 130 ABY (Allies, p10–31; and the p163–166 flashback): Roan Fel and his cousin Mohrgan with the Imperial Knights; Krayt's coup; Maladi as Krayt's agent; teen Cade adrift after Ossus, picked up by Rav's pirates.
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:36`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Visions (framing, not feats): Mara Jade, Anakin Skywalker / Darth Vader (p129–134) and Luke Skywalker (p168) appear to a drugged or meditating Cade.
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:38`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): #9: Maladi breaks the Jedi healer Hosk Trey'lis with interrogator droids and her will (p58–60). Krayt wants Cade "ALIVE"; Maladi doesn't know why (p61). Calixte tells Veed that Maladi's top agent is "JOR TOLIN" (sic; Torlin elsewhere), Sith-raised but lacking "SUFFICIENT FORCE SENSITIVITY", and reactivates "Morrigan Corde" (p62). She spots Cade on the Wheel's security vids (p63–64). On Bastion, Fel punishes Draco and Krieg for their rescue mission with medals and the most dangerous assignments, and sends them to escort Capt. Bovark to GA Admiral Gar Stazi on the Wheel (p65–67). Talks begin (p68–70). The two Knights sense the GA guards' concealed blasters (p71). On the Wheel's lower levels, Torlin fights a gang that Corde set on him. Corde joins in, Torlin strangles her, and she shocks him unconscious (p72–79).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p59 Maladi: "YOUR WILL IS NO MATCH FOR MINE"](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p059-maladi-will-breaks-hosk.jpg)
![p62 "JOR TOLIN" dossier](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p062-jor-tolin-dossier.jpg)
![p65 Fel: "WE PUT FIVE OF THEM ON HIS SHIP, KRIEG."](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p065-fel-five-homing-devices.jpg)
![p71 the Knights on the Wheel](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p071-knights-sense-ga-guards.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:52`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): #10: Corde interrogates and releases Torlin (p80–83). They spot the talks and use six C.O.L.D. limpet droids to make the Imperial shuttle fire on the GA shuttle (p84–88). Stazi knifes Bovark. Draco Force-pushes Stazi, and Draco and Krieg deflect the guards' fire and get Bovark out. Pol Temm bans Fel's forces from the Wheel (p89–92). On Ossus, Torlin turns on Corde ("THIS IS 'LATER.'"). They fight a sniper duel, and Corde kills him with a nerve toxin she implanted while he was unconscious (p93–98). A holo and flashback reveal she is Cade's mother; she leaves Ossus without seeing him (p98–101).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:53`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): #11: On Ossus, Cade overdoses on death sticks and has visions of Mara Jade and of Anakin/Vader, who runs him through in the vision (p128–134). K'Kruhk, who is real, heals him (p135–137). Sazen and Shado arrive, and Cade asks to finish his training (p137–138). On Socorro, Rav sells Jariah and Deliah to the Empire. Jariah won't give up Cade (p139–142). Two weeks later, sparring shows Cade has "RAW POWER" but atrophied skills (p143). Sazen tells K'Kruhk of his vision of a Skywalker reuniting the Jedi; K'Kruhk fears "A NEW VADER" (p143–145). Cade hides from Shado's Force sense in the tunnels. They fall into a hidden chamber of Jedi artifacts, and Yuuzhan Vong warriors attack (p145–149).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p136 K'Kruhk heals Cade](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p136-kkruhk-heals-cade.jpg)
![p143 "RAW POWER ... FINER SKILLS HAVE ATROPHIED"](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p143-raw-power-atrophied.jpg)
![p145 Cade hides from Shado's Force sense](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p145-cade-hides-from-force.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:61`

> WITHIN THE FLAMES, HIS BODY DISAPPEARED.
>
> IF HE EVER GOES COMPLETELY OVER TO THE DARK SIDE, I WILL END HIM. *(p170)*
>
> NO ONE DIES FOR ME. NEVER AGAIN. *(p171)*
— Source: Legacy Volume 2, *Shards* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): #12: A Vong warrior pins Cade. Master Shaper Nei Rin stops the fight, and Sazen and K'Kruhk arrive (p150–151). Nei Rin explains the Ossus Project, the Sith sabotage, and Kol's funeral: "WITHIN THE FLAMES, HIS BODY DISAPPEARED." She offers Cade the Jedi legacy, and he refuses it (p152–155). At the spot where Kol died, Cade attacks Sazen and loses. Sazen tells him his healings drew on the dark side and calls Kol's last stand a selfless holding action (p156–161). In the meditation of emptiness, Cade remembers drifting in space and his rescue by Rav and Jariah (p162–166). He then sees the tortured Hosk, and Luke's spirit tells him to make it right (p167–168). Nei Rin gives him Kol's lightsaber, now with a Vong-shaped lambent crystal, and K'Kruhk gives him R2-D2 (p169–170). Shado: "IF HE EVER GOES COMPLETELY OVER TO THE DARK SIDE, I WILL END HIM." (p170). Cade heads for Coruscant: "NO ONE DIES FOR ME. NEVER AGAIN." (p171).
![p150 a Vong warrior pins Cade](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p150-vong-pin-cade.jpg)
![p154 Nei Rin: "KOL SKYWALKER WAS YOUR GREATEST WARRIOR"](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p154-nei-rin-greatest-warrior.jpg)
![p159 Sazen on Cade's dark-side healing](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p159-sazen-vs-cade-dark-side-healing.jpg)
![p160 Sazen throws Cade: "TOGETHER WE ARE STRONGER"](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p160-sazen-flips-cade.jpg)
![p161 Sazen on Kol's last stand](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p161-kol-could-only-hold-off-sith.jpg)
![p168 Luke's spirit and Hosk](012-legacy-vol-2-shards-panels/p168-luke-ghost-hosk.jpg)
Our notes: `notes/materials/012-legacy-vol-2-shards.md:62`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): From stasis, Krayt senses "SKYWALKER IS COMING" while Wyyrlok fears he is dying (p10). Cade shakes off a four-ship Skull Squadron flight in Coruscant traffic and hides his ship in the undercity (p11–14, p22). He leaves the *Mynock*'s codes with Queen Jool at Rik's cantina for Syn and Deliah (p17–21). Gunner Yage is dressed down by her father (p22–23). Cade slips into the Sith Temple, finds Hosk with the Force and frees him (p24–27). Talon ambushes them; Cade has the better of the first exchange until Nihl strikes him from behind (p27–30). Sazen senses Cade's darkness from Ossus (p31).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
![p10 Wyyrlok: "MY LORD IS DYING"; Krayt: "SKYWALKER IS COMING."](013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon-panels/p010-wyyrlok-my-lord-is-dying.jpg)

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Maladi calls off the Imperial search; the Moffs squabble, and Calixte, Veed and the Yages trade barbs (p32–36). Maladi tortures Cade with ixetal cilona, which he keeps purging; Krayt arrives (p37–38). Talon brings in Syn and Deliah; Maladi triggers coral seeds in them, Krayt frees one of Cade's hands with the Force, and Cade heals both at once with dark-side power (p39–45). Cade trades his freedom for theirs (p45–46). Calixte and Veed plan to use Corde (p47–48). Wyyrlok keeps Nihl and Maladi outside; they argue over birth and rank (p49–50). Over dinner Krayt shows Cade Kol's encased lightsaber and reveals he was the Jedi A'Sharad Hett (p51–53).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:44`

- Note (summary from our notes, not a quotation): Corde gets past Veed's security, demands three dozen Vong bugs and a stand-down "ON MY SIGNAL" (p54). At Rik's she recruits Syn, Deliah, Chak and Kee and tells them she is Cade's mother (p55–59). Krayt tells his story: Clone Wars, Order 66, the Tuskens, and his defeat and maiming by Kenobi (p60–69). Cade mocks him, Talon throws him with the Force, and Cade blasts her across the room and threatens to "EXPLODE" her weak point; Krayt stops him, cuts his cuffs, orders him to heal her and makes Talon his teacher; Cade asks to be taught "THE WAYS OF THE SITH" (p70–73). On Ossus the Jedi hide from Stryfe's troopers (p74–75).
  — Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Our notes: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md:48`

> THAT WHICH CAN HEAL CAN ALSO BREAK. *(p115)*
— Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Cade hangs in the Vong "EMBRACE OF PAIN" while Krayt tells the rest: Vong capture, shaping, Vergere, his vision, the escape, the new Order, Lumiya and "CADEUS", stasis, and his dying body (p98–107). Cade slips out of his cell; the crew launches (p108–109). Talon is waiting in Maladi's lab and drops him (p110). Krayt orders him to kill Hosk; Cade refuses, Hosk forgives everyone, and Krayt runs Hosk through; Hosk's body vanishes (p111–114). Kol's ghost: "THAT WHICH CAN HEAL CAN ALSO BREAK." (p115). Cade's telekinetic burst shatters the case, he calls Kol's saber, strikes Talon and engages Nihl while Krayt urges revenge (p116–119).

Note (our notes, around the quote): Corde rocketpacks up the Temple; Syn's bugs blow the main hangar (p120–122), the "signal" Veed recognises (p123). Cade beats Nihl but refuses to kill him "BECAUSE YOU WANT ME TO!" and attacks Krayt (p124–126). Skull Squadron is ordered off (p127, p130–131). Krayt boasts and Cade taunts him about the Vong growths (p128–129); Krayt has Cade on the floor when Corde shoots him through the window (p132). Cade jumps from the tower and Syn catches him in the *Mynock* (p133–135). Corde tells Cade she is his mother and leaves by the undercity (p136). Krayt: "I WILL BREAK HIM AND I WILL USE HIM AND THEN I WILL KILL HIM." (p137). Veed and Calixte discuss the outcome; alone, Calixte plays a hologram of Kol, herself and toddler Cade: "RUN." (p138–141).

> “Superbly,” he admitted.
>
> “Superbly,” he admitted.
>
> “Then reward me! Make me your apprentice! Teach me the ways of the Sith!”
>
> “Have I not taught you many secrets, Asajj?”
>
> “Have I not taught you many secrets, Asajj?”
>
> “Have I not taught you many secrets, Asajj?”
>
> “Scraps. Little devices. Lesser arts. Not nearly what you would if I were your apprentice sworn in blood, I know. I am no fool,” she said angrily. As if he didn’t know that. As if she needed to convince him she was deadly. “I have learned much about the Sith. Their lineage and their greatness.”
>
> “But what of their natural history?” Dooku said.
>
> Ventress blinked. “What?”
>
> “The Sith, considered as a species. An insect, perhaps.”
>
> Asajj’s thin lips got thinner. “You mock me.”
>
> “I have rarely been more serious.” The Count paced over to a shelf of holocrons on the wall, plucked one out, and inserted it into the comm cube on his desk. “Behold: the sickle-back mantis of Dantooine.” A glowing picture formed in the air over the desk, a glossy red-and-black mantis, all hooked forelimbs and wicked piety. “After mating, the female tears her partner’s head off and lays her eggs in his body. When the broodlings hatch, they eat their way out and then attack one another.”
>
> “I am not given to parables,” Ventress said impatiently. “If you have a point, make it.”
>
> “It is a tricky business, this making of apprentices,” Dooku said. “The true Sith Lord must find a pupil in whom the Force runs strong.”
>
> “Sixteen Jedi dead is some testament to that,” Ventress said. “Should have been seventeen,” she added.
>
> “But do I really want to make you so strong?” the Count said softly. “We are such pleasant company now, while you know your place. But if I were to make you my apprentice, if I were to take you by the hand and lead you down below the black water that is the dark side, then either you would drown, or you would grow far stronger, and neither option appeals to me. You burn so brightly now, I would hate to put you out.”
>
> “Why should you? What harm is there in teaching me to help you better?”
>
> “You would betray me.” He shrugged, cutting off her protests. “It is the unhappy hazard of embracing the dark side. I am old, and I have learned the limits of my ambition. You are young, and strong, and those two things have always led to one place in the history of the Sith.”
>
> “You think I would intrigue against you?”
>
> “Not at first. But a day would come when you would disagree with my decisions. When you would start to dream of how much better things would be without my liver-spotted hand held over you.”
>
> “I disagree with your decisions right now,” she said. “About that Jedi who—”
>
> “Should have been number seventeen. I know.” Dooku smiled. “I don’t have your appetites. I can wait on my kills, and use them better. And for now, you might disagree, but you dare not disobey.” And here, with a small smile, he lifted just one finger.
>
> She blanched. “True,” she said.
>
> Dooku let his finger drop.
>
> In the hologram on the desk, baby mantises were squirming from their father’s body. They groped blindly about them with their spindly hooked limbs until one, a little larger than the others, chanced to find that the sickles on his hind legs fit like a collar around a sibling’s neck. Driven by primitive instinct, he jerked and tore off his brother’s head.
>
> “In a perfect world,” Dooku said, “one could feed an apprentice just enough to keep him growing—just enough to keep him wanting more. The Master could promise him fame, glamour. That’s a good one to deliver on,” he said. “He could do the Master’s bidding, be his public face. Then if any of the Master’s plans went wrong, why, he could take the fall.” Dooku looked up, his eyes suddenly sharp and very much in the present. “Does that sound good to you, Asajj? Would you truly like to be my apprentice? I could make you the most feared woman in the galaxy. All the Jedi would come looking for you, while I sat safe and sound in Coruscant, biding my time.”
— Source: Legacy Volume 3, *Claws of the Dragon* (TPB): power-scaling notes with panels; ![p73 Cade heals Talon.
(Excerpt widened 2026-11-17 to the relevant passage, word for word from Yoda (Sean Stewart) (text copy `MEGA_Legend_The_Clone_Wars_Series_Star_Wars_Clone_Wars_20_Yoda_Dark_Rendezvous_Sean_Stewart_epub`).)

Note (our notes, around the quote): _Added by the material 013 pass on 2026-10-21. Nothing above this heading was changed. Every quote was read from the page images of the *Legacy Volume 3: Claws of the Dragon* TPB (Legacy #14–19; 146-page scan), with lettering kept in capitals. "p" is the TPB's PDF page; the matching omnibus (009) page is p+301 throughout the story pages (p8–141). Issue numbers are inferred from 22-page issues (#14 p10–31, #15 p32–53, #16 p54–75, #17 p76–97, #18 p98–119, #19 p120–141), so they are marked ≈. Only items not already in the 009 appendix are listed. Panels and full context: `notes/materials/013-legacy-vol-3-claws-of-the-dragon.md`. Corrections to the 009 appendix above (not edited there): (a) the 009 p338 purge scene's torturer is Darth Maladi, not Talon (Krayt's p339 line is still Krayt's); (b) Krayt's "STRIKE ME DOWN! … NOTHING LESS WILL EVEN MAKE ME PAUSE!" (009 p413 = 013 p112) names no addressee; Cade is holding the offered saber, so it is most likely aimed at Cade rather than Hosk; (c) in the Claws tower fight Krayt has Cade on the floor when Corde's shot hits him (p132). The 009 note is now corrected in the same way._

# Debate and forum claims (unverified)

Forum posts, Suspect Insight debates and Discord debates, respect threads, and verdicts. These are posters' arguments or quotations they typed; none is checked against the book here unless the entry says so.

## Star Wars Saga Edition: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels

> RPG stats place post-Sith-training Cade below Draco
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: *Legacy Era Campaign Guide* (WotC 2009): power-scaling notes with panels.
Note (our notes, around the quote): #17 (Draco): the Draco quote in verdicts.md is verified (above). On "RPG stats place post-Sith-training Cade below Draco": the guide's Cade (p210, CL11) is explicitly the version who has just escaped the Coruscant Sith Temple (p209 bio), i.e. *during and just after* his Sith training. So the claim is literally true for that version, and RNG's counter (it doesn't depict the Cade who later dominated Draco) is also correct.
Posted by: Draco.
Our notes: `notes/materials/011-legacy-era-campaign-guide.md:1083`

Note (our notes, around the quote): Proposal 2 (Krayt underestimates him): possible. Even so, Krayt "cannot 'easily' defeat Cade". Cade has to be dangerous to "a not-particularly-trying Krayt". The Skywalker X+10 jump is "baked in", so Talon must fear X+20. Ant also cites "flashes of brilliance", the shuttle thrown at Talon, the gutting, Dark Transfer, and Claws: "THE ONLY THING THAT SAVES YOU THUS FAR IS THE BLOOD OF VADER…"
Posted by: Krayt underestimates him.
Our notes: `notes/materials/016-dissecting-legacy-3-cade-vs-krayt.md:34`

Note (our notes, around the quote): Kill → bend → break → join (p46). "Kill" means sending Cade as near death as possible, echoing Krayt's own death ("EVERY NERVE FIBER ABLAZE", "YAWNING ABYSS OF THE GRAVE", "ASTRIDE THE GRAVE", p2–3). So it is "goal optimizing" to "unload everything on Cade".

> If not for Antares Draco stopping the virus, which Krayt did not foresee or anticipate, Fel would have unleashed the virus before Krayt had actually defeated Cade… if Krayt could readily defeat Cade, he would have. The fact he didn't reveals he couldn't.
> [fragment — full passage not on file]
— Source: Cade Skywalker vs Darth Krayt" (Suspect Insight Forums) — https://www.suspectinsightforums.com/t4457-dissecting-legacy-3-cade-skywalker-vs-darth-krayt; Omega Red time pressure. The toxin kills.
Note (our notes, around the quote): Omega Red time pressure. The toxin kills "within a matter of breaths" (p108), and Sith are immune. It probably keys on dark-side energy, and Cade is still "on the edge of light and dark" (p123), so he might not survive it. Krayt therefore has to finish fast. "If not for Antares Draco stopping the virus, which Krayt did not foresee or anticipate, Fel would have unleashed the virus before Krayt had actually defeated Cade… if Krayt could readily defeat Cade, he would have. The fact he didn't reveals he couldn't."

Note (our notes, around the quote): This blog (September 1st) follows Master Azronger's addendum in Material 019 (August 23rd). That addendum claimed Krayt "was maintaining telepathic contact with 'millions' of his Sith Troopers… more of an active than passive effort". It added that before War his mind was open only to his "closest disciples", and that Cade and Stryfe felt him leave their heads after Had Abaddon. The "long-standing proposals" Ant rebuts here are those claims, so 018 is effectively Ant's answer to Azronger.

> Krayt plans to “kill” Cade and wants him to “experience” “death.” He monologues that he has had a vision that reveals even before Cade “bends” and “breaks” that “first” he “must die.” So, Krayt’s plan sequence is: “kill” Cade -> bend Cade -> break Cade -> join with Cade.
>
> What does “kill” mean? Krayt wants Cade to experience what he did. Krayt says he remembers the “yawning abyss of the grave waiting to suck me all the way in,” which taken literally is likely referencing the pull of Chaos against Krayt’s spirit. Krayt says at this pivotal juncture, he found a “secret” that allowed him to “astride” (or straddle) the grave. From there, Krayt fought his way back to life. So, by “kill,” Krayt means he plans to send Cade as close to death as possible. And the more pain, the merrier, since then it should be more reflective of Krayt’s own death experience, in which he remembers “every nerve fiber ablaze.”
— Source: //www.suspectinsightforums.com/t4459-debunking-darthant66-s-dissecting-legacy-series.

Note (our notes, around the quote): | E9 | Omega Red forces haste [016 #4] | No evidence; foresight (p116); Maladi plant; "idea"; Krayt can revive a corpse | Krayt can't revive a corpse; he drove his spirit within (B034 = p4). Cade's death would ruin the plan ("serve at my side", "until I become dust"). Krayt lured Cade to drop the shields and expected to beat him before the toxin dropped. Draco stalled Fel. "He doesn't even make the original deadline." | ILS on the main point; Ant on the corpse | Ant is right that p4 shows self-preservation, not revival. Ant is also right that the p46 and p123 plan needs Cade alive. But nothing on-page says Krayt lured Cade *to drop the shields* (the defence mission is the allies' own plan, p100–102), or that Krayt had a deadline. p108 ("PROMISES KEPT. IT IS DONE, MY LORD KRAYT.") and p116 ("AS I FORESAW") support ILS's "planned and foreseen". |

That’s no different from your attempt to prove the decreases were miniscule: analyses of his showings. An explicit statement doesn’t have to be provided for everything.
>
> Also, you blatantly don’t understand how the coral seeds function. They drain Krayt’s Force connection, and he holds off the infestation with the Force. So the more the infestation spreads, the lesser Krayt’s ability to ward it off becomes, and so he has to devote a bigger portion of his reserves to keep it at bay to prevent it from spreading faster. So not only is his Force power lesser 7 years after Legacy # 1, but his ability to use that power in combat is also lesser.
>
> Prove to me Krayt was remotely serious in his last match with Cade. He begins by contemptuously tossing Cade aside with telekinesis and ragdolls his ass around as indicated by the blue trail his hand leaves. He sends a few sparks of lightning Cade’s way which, from what we see, don’t even touch Cade, so there is no way to know how Cade dealt with them. Then the fight goes on off-panel, meaning we have no idea what transpired during that time; your assertion that Cade and Krayt “duel for a significant period of time” is baseless.* All we see is that they are dueling **after** it; they could have started as soon as Krayt got done with the lightning or only a few seconds before we resume on their duel.

> **[[****Cade Skywalker casually throws a rather big ship a great distance (**Legacy # 5 [LINK: http://suspectinsight.forumotion.com/gallery/Star-Wars/Legacy-Era/ship-scaling-1-pic_335.htm]**). He did this while high on death sticks, which dampened his connection to the Force (**Legacy # 3 [LINK: http://suspectinsight.forumotion.com/gallery/Star-Wars/Legacy-Era/ship-scaling-4-pic_338.htm]**). The Jedi then remove the death sticks out of his system and re-train him in the ways of the Force (**Legacy # 11 [LINK: https://readcomiconline.to/Comic/Star-Wars-Legacy-2006/Issue-11?id=12290]**). Cade also receives additional training from the Sith (**Legacy # 17 [LINK: https://readcomiconline.to/Comic/Star-Wars-Legacy-2006/Issue-17?id=12296]**). He is put into the Embrace of Pain where he learns to focus his anger into a weapon of the dark side (**Legacy # 18 [LINK: http://suspectinsight.forumotion.com/gallery/Star-Wars/Legacy-Era/ship-scaling-5-pic_339.htm]**), as can be seen from his prior failure and consequent success to perceive just how messed up Darth Krayt’s body truly is (**Legacy # 19 [LINK: http://suspectinsight.forumotion.com/gallery/Star-Wars/Legacy-Era/ship-scaling-6-pic_340.htm]**). He should thus be far more powerful when he got stalemated by Darth Stryfe in a telekinetic war than when he tossed the ship (**Legacy # 31 [LINK: http://suspectinsight.forumotion.com/gallery/Star-Wars/Legacy-Era/cade-stalemates-stryfe-pic_270.htm]**). The gap between Stryfe and Krayt is detailed in my third post to ArkhamAsylum3.****]]**

## Arcann (XSupremeSkillzX) vs Vong Krayt (Azronger)

> Enough about me. Let’s talk about you. You and my dad opened my eyes to my abilities. I can see real clear now. I can see how sick and twisted up your insides are. I can see what fighting me is costing you. Those vong...things in your innards. Nasty little critters want to take you over real bad. What do they feel like, huh? Something gnawing your guts? Clawing at you? Tell me--do the other Sith know how sick you really are? How weak? What would they do if they knew?

> She raised her free hand above her head and fired off another burst of lightning. Both Scourge and Meetra threw themselves clear of the deadly electrical bolt, but in doing so they gave Nyriss the early advantage.
>
> Before they could recover, she leapt at them. Despite her withered appearance, she moved with the all the speed and ferocity of a dark side warrior in her prime. She landed right between her two adversaries, her blade flashing back and forth in a series of slashes and cuts that immediately threw her two opponents on the defensive.
>
> Scourge barely managed to parry the first wave of her assault, unable to even think about countering with an attack of his own. Another quick thrust forced him off balance and he staggered backward.
>
> Nyriss seized on the opportunity to focus all her efforts on breaking through Meetra’s defenses. The Jedi was clearly overmatched; though she managed to hold her ground, she was forced down to one knee.
>
> In the awkward position her right flank was exposed, and Nyriss brought her blade in to deliver a crippling cut. At the same time, Scourge lashed out with the Force, catching Nyriss flush in the center of her chest.
>
> An ordinary foe would have been thrown clear across the room, but Nyriss instinctively threw up a Force barrier to protect herself, absorbing and redirecting the brunt of the impact. Even so, Scourge’s attack knocked her off balance just enough to send her lightsaber wide of the mark, giving Meetra the opportunity she needed to scramble away to safety.
>
> Scourge rushed forward, hoping to drive Nyriss back into a corner, but she met his charge with an invisible wave of rippling energy. It picked Scourge up and tossed him head-over-heels, sending him crashing to the wall.
>
> Dazed, he looked up just in time to see another bolt of violet lightning catch Meetra in the chest. Like Nyriss, she threw up a barrier to save herself from the worst of it, but she was still knocked from her feet.
>
> “Did you think I would be as easy to defeat as Xedrix?” Nyriss shouted, raising her lightsaber triumphantly above her head.
>
> The air around her began to crackle and grow hot as she gathered herself for the killing blow. Scourge felt the energy building inside her, and he knew he would be powerless to stop it. Nyriss was too powerful; her command of the dark side was too strong.
>
> “Gaze upon me and see your doom!” she declared. “I am Darth Nyriss, Lord of the Sith. I am the conqueror of Drezzi, the destroyer of Melldia, and a member of the Dark Council!”
>
> Scourge braced himself for the end.
>
> Just then, Revan emerged from the cell. He had pulled the hood of his Jedi robe up to cover his head, and he wore the red-and-gray mask, hiding his face.
>
> A dozen bolts of lightning sprang from Nyriss’s hand, arcing across the room to incinerate her enemies. Instead of leaping back into the cell to avoid the deadly attack, Revan stepped forward to intercept it.

> In Legacy #5, Cade throws a rather sizeable spacecraft a considerable distance; in red is indicated the height of the ship and in blue is its width, so you may see from the bottom scan just how far he tossed it. He did this while high on death sticks, which dampened his connection to the Force (Legacy # 3 ). The Jedi then remove the death sticks out of his system and re-train him in the ways of the Force (Legacy # 11 ). Cade also receives additional training from the Sith (Legacy # 17 ). He is put into the Embrace of Pain where he learns to focus his anger into a weapon of the dark side (Legacy # 18 ), as can be seen from his prior failure and consequent success to perceive just how messed up Darth Krayt’s body truly is (Legacy # 19 ). He should thus be far more powerful when he got stalemated by Darth Stryfe in a telekinetic war than when he tossed the ship (Legacy # 31 ). Stryfe - Cade’s equal - was utterly dominated by Saarai (Legacy # 34 ), whose own powers were "nascent" in comparison to Darth Wyyrlok III (Legacy: War # 1), who himself has his dark side abilities "surpassed" (Insider # 88) and his skill in lightning and telekinesis "far outstripped" (Insider # 113) by Vong Krayt.
